# KNOuX SmartOrganizer — Ultimate 181-Service Specification
## Volumes 6–7: Evidence Reconciliation, Resolved Technical Determinations and the 181-Row Acceptance Matrix

Authored for the Codex implementation agent · Dated 2026-11-03 · Continues Volumes 1–5 (Volume 1 file: Sections 1–22; Volumes 4–5 file: Sections 23–31)

---

## 32. Status, Authority and How to Read This Volume

### 32.1 What this volume is

Volumes 1–5 were written before any repository material was available, so almost every statement about current implementation carried the label NOT VERIFIED. The owner has since supplied the evidence archive `KNOuX_CLAUDE_EVIDENCE_181_20261103_001923.zip` and the authoring directive. This volume does three things:

1. **Reconciles** the ledgers in the archive against each other and against Volumes 1–5, and replaces NOT VERIFIED with an evidence-backed status wherever the archive supports one (Sections 33–35 and the matrix in Section 40).
2. **Resolves the technical questions** that Volumes 4–5 deferred to inspection and that the directive asked to be settled without bothering the owner: the WinGet interface (Section 36), Google OAuth scopes and verification (Section 37) and capability-driven hardware sensors (Section 38).
3. **Closes Volume 6**: the 181-row acceptance matrix (Section 40), a per-service evidence addendum with acceptance tests and completion evidence for every entry (Section 41), and the consolidated blocker list (Section 42).

Volumes 1–5 remain authoritative for every per-service contract (the 12-part template, objectives, backend/frontend contracts and acceptance lines). This volume does not rewrite them. Where this volume and an earlier volume disagree about **current state**, this volume wins; where they disagree about **required behavior**, the stricter requirement wins and the disagreement is listed in Section 33.

### 32.2 Evidence classes used here

| Class | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **LEDGER** | Stated in a file of the evidence archive (path and file given). |
| **DIRECTIVE** | Stated by the owner in the authoring directive for this session (for example the WinGet implementation facts). |
| **OFFICIAL** | Read from an official vendor page during this authoring session (URL given in Section 43). |
| **KNOWLEDGE** | Platform knowledge of the author that was **not** re-read from an official page this session. Always paired with an instruction to confirm during implementation. |
| **NOT VERIFIED** | Only the source tree, a test run or a real Windows host can establish it. |

### 32.3 What the archive does and does not contain

The archive holds nine documents, a pack manifest and a readme: `LIVE_SERVICE_EXECUTION_STATUS_181.csv`, `SERVICE_WORKSPACE_IMPLEMENTATION.csv`, `SERVICE_CAPABILITY_MATRIX.md`, `SERVICE_REGISTRY_EVIDENCE.md`, `SERVICE_WORKBENCH_ACCEPTANCE.md`, `SERVICE_WORKBENCH_HOST_AUDIT.md`, `MISSION_LEDGER.json`, `missions/REAL_SERVICE_FUNCTIONALITY_STANDARD.md` and `docs/handoffs/VISUAL_FIDELITY_CODEX_20261102.md`. It contains **no source tree, no test logs and no screenshots**. Consequences, applied throughout:

- Every file path below is a path **named by a ledger**, not a path the author opened. Codex must open it before relying on it.
- The directive states the existence and names of the WinGet functions in `apps/gemini-ui/electron/native/app-bridge.cjs`. Their bodies, argument arrays, timeouts and tests are NOT VERIFIED.
- Any evidence path cited by a ledger (for example `docs/evidence/studio-fullstack/verified-packaged/proof.json`) is **cited, not inspected**. Codex re-reads it before treating a layer as proven.
- The readme of the archive states that the CSV holds **181 registered entries, not 181 verified complete engines**, and that the pack is a mixed historical/current snapshot. This volume honors that: no row is promoted beyond what a named record supports.

### 32.5 Precedence rule when sources disagree

The archive's own `MISSION_LEDGER.json` names `docs/LIVE_SERVICE_EXECUTION_STATUS_181.csv` as the source of truth for the 181 IDs. Applying that, in descending order:

1. `current_slice_status` with an `implementation_sha` and cited evidence paths (LIVE ledger).
2. A specific `baseline_status` in the LIVE ledger (for example `BLOCKED — fixture preview only; …`).
3. `SERVICE_REGISTRY_EVIDENCE.md` status for the mapped registry ID (dated 2026-10-26; partly amended 2026-10-27 and 2026-11-02).
4. `SERVICE_WORKSPACE_IMPLEMENTATION.csv` status. Its `provider_native_binding` text "Existing contract retained; not newly audited" means **no audit happened**; it is an assertion, not evidence.
5. `SERVICE_WORKBENCH_ACCEPTANCE.md` (oldest; explicitly "NOT TEST EVIDENCE").

Nothing in this order proves a Windows-native, packaged or owner-data layer. A status is only as high as the highest layer for which an evidence record exists (Volume 1, Section 10.1).

### 33.2 Identifier reconciliation (REQUIRED)

Volumes 1–5 use spec references `D<domain>.<nn>`. The ledgers use catalog IDs. The mapping is positional within each domain, and it was checked name-by-name for all 181 rows (the 181 service names in the ledger equal the 181 headings in Volumes 1–5, in order). The full mapping is Section 40.

For the 18 registry-only IDs, the following **proposed** aliases are derived from identical service names. Codex confirms each against the installed UI service registry before relying on it; a wrong alias is recorded in the reconciliation table, not silently fixed.

| Ledger ID (spec ref) | Proposed registry ID | | Ledger ID (spec ref) | Proposed registry ID |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mission-04-01 (D04.01) | google-sheets | | mission-14-01 (D14.01) | settings-engine |
| mission-04-02 (D04.02) | google-gmail | | mission-14-02 (D14.02) | settings-ai-copilot |
| mission-04-03 (D04.03) | google-tasks | | mission-14-03 (D14.03) | settings-workspace |
| mission-04-04 (D04.04) | google-forms | | mission-14-04 (D14.04) | settings-guards |
| mission-04-05 (D04.05) | google-contacts | | mission-14-05 (D14.05) | settings-appearance |
| mission-04-06 (D04.06) | google-calendar | | mission-14-06 (D14.06) | settings-language |
| mission-04-07 (D04.07) | google-picker | | mission-14-07 (D14.07) | settings-backup |
| mission-04-08 (D04.08) | google-slides | | mission-14-08 (D14.08) | settings-assistant |
| mission-04-09 (D04.09) | google-chat | | | |
| mission-04-10 (D04.10) | google-classroom | | | |

Note a name collision to resolve deliberately: D14.03 "Google Workspace Hub" (registry `settings-workspace`) is the connection hub; D04.01–D04.10 are the ten Google services. D14.03 owns connect/disconnect/revoke and the token vault; each D04 service owns its own API adapter and consent summary. Neither duplicates the other's code.

### 33.4 Status vocabulary mapping

The archive uses its own vocabulary (`MISSION_LEDGER.json.statusVocabulary`: COMPLETE_VERIFIED, PARTIAL, BLOCKED_EXTERNAL, UNVERIFIED, PLANNED) and the CSVs use MEASURED_READ_ONLY, PARTIAL, GATED, UNSUPPORTED, UNASSESSED, CONNECTABLE_AWAITING_CONSENT, BLOCKED, IMPLEMENTED_AND_TESTED_FIXTURES. Volume 1 Section 8.2 defines the vocabulary this specification uses. The mapping applied in Section 40:

| Archive value | Section 8.2 value | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `IMPLEMENTED_AND_TESTED_FIXTURES` (current slice, with SHA) | Implemented and fixture tested | Never "runtime verified": the ledger's `remaining_gate` lists open owner/packaged/signed gates |
| `PARTIAL` | Partially implemented | |
| `MEASURED_READ_ONLY`, `READ_ONLY` | Read-only by intended design | Class A observation; complete when it observes accurately and states what it cannot observe; remains **historical** until re-verified |
| `UNSUPPORTED` | Not implemented | Unsupported by design, platform or missing provider; controls intentionally off |
| `CONNECTABLE_AWAITING_CONSENT`, `GATED` | Gated by user consent | |
| `BLOCKED — fixture preview only…` | Preview-only despite requiring execution | |
| `BLOCKED_REQUIRES_USER_ACTION` | Blocked by external provider | |
| `BLOCKED — session-only key; no native vault/proxy` | Not implemented | Vault/proxy absent; external key also needed |
| `AUDIT_REQUIRED` + `UNASSESSED` | Not verified | |

## 34. Domain Evidence Digest (what the ledgers say exists today)

Source: `SERVICE_WORKSPACE_IMPLEMENTATION.csv` (assertions; "not newly audited" means no audit) and the LIVE ledger. Paths are named by the ledger and must be opened before use. Operation IDs and IPC channel names are **not in the archive**; they live in `apps/gemini-ui/shared/native-contracts.ts` (named by Volume 1) and Codex fills them into Section 40 by reading that file.

### D01 — Home Command Center (12 entries)
- **Provider / binding** (12 of 12): Existing contract retained; not newly audited
- **UI** (12 of 12): Existing specialized renderer retained; shared materials changed where applicable; individual acceptance deferred
- **Gap / dependency** (12 of 12): Continue domain activation against original spec
- **Verification recorded** (12 of 12): NOT_RUN_FINAL_ACCEPTANCE; prior evidence remains historical
- **Files named**: `src/visual/service-workspaces.css`, `src/visual/visual-sprint.css`

### D02 — Smart Scan (10 entries)
- **Provider / binding** (9 of 10): Existing Smart Scan coordinator, typed native IPC and persisted session model unchanged
- **Provider / binding** (1 of 10): Existing ScanBridge measured files -> current-source/session validation -> existing Studio picker/fresh scan/immutable plan/native consent/move-copy/receipt/Undo
- **UI** (9 of 10): Existing dedicated renderer retained; in-tool folder authorization/indexing, native cancellation, measured evidence copy, visible failures; specialized filters added for duplicate/large/junk/startup/app tools

## 35. Shared Acceptance Test Catalog (referenced by ID from every matrix row)

Volume 1 Section 10.3 lists the required test *categories*. This section gives them stable IDs so that a matrix row can say exactly which tests prove it. Every test records command, exit code, counts, tested SHA, environment and fixture-versus-real label (Volume 1 Section 10.2). A test that cannot run in the current environment is recorded `NOT RUN` with the reason and is never reported as passed.

### 35.1 Universal tests (every service)

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| U-1 | Dead-control check | Every visible control either executes a real operation or is disabled with a truthful reason string; no control is decorative |
| U-2 | Honest states | Each of the 13 workspace states in Volume 1 Section 8.6 that applies renders distinctly; no fabricated percentage, count, timeline point or demo row |
| U-3 | Contract compatibility | Renderer ↔ preload ↔ IPC schema test passes; `native-contracts.ts` changes are additive; sender validation rejects an unknown sender |
| U-4 | Error classification | Provider-missing, access-denied, timeout, malformed provider output and unexpected exception each yield a distinct typed error, never an empty success |
| U-5 | UI matrix | Day/Night, EN/AR with RTL/LTR, 1366×768 / 1672×941 / 1920×1080, keyboard-only with visible focus, accessible names, reduced motion; Living Glass identity preserved. Visual approval remains Codex's stream and the owner's gate 10 |
| U-6 | Regression guard | Existing tests for the service (and its shared engines) still pass on the exact SHA |
| U-7 | No secret / path leakage | Logs and exports contain no tokens, keys, Wi-Fi keys, proxy passwords; path and identity redaction follows the log-privacy policy |

### 35.2 Class A — observation

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| OBS-1 | Ground truth | Provider output on a fixture equals independently computed truth; the same on at least one real Windows host, labeled as such |
| OBS-2 | Provenance | Every metric carries value, unit, sample time (OS-supplied where available; otherwise read time flagged), provider and source |
| OBS-3 | Freshness | A stopped or slow sampler shows the last real sample with an age marker; no value appears before its first real sample |
| OBS-4 | Capability honesty | Absent, denied, unsupported and needs-elevation each render their own state; unknown is "—" with a reason, never 0 |
| OBS-5 | Cancellation | Cancel stops the walker, query or child process (process tree killed), the session ends `cancelled`, and a re-read shows no orphan |
| OBS-6 | Bounded work | Result-size, time and memory bounds are enforced; truncation is stated in the UI |
| OBS-7 | Export | Exports equal the displayed data; sensitive fields follow redaction defaults |

### 35.3 Class B — reversible action

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| ACT-1 | Plan binding | Execution accepts only a plan identified by an immutable hash of its exact contents; changing any element invalidates approval |
| ACT-2 | Consent | Approval is operation-specific, recorded with time and plan hash, and cannot be pre-granted for later operations |
| ACT-3 | Stale-plan rejection | A target that changed since preview (size, mtime, identity, owner, state) is rejected and reported |
| ACT-4 | Execution with live progress | Progress derives from completed units, never a timer; counts equal the journal |
| ACT-5 | Postcondition verification | After the operation, an independent read proves the intended effect and the absence of unrelated mutation (before/after manifest diff) |
| ACT-6 | Journal | Every operation writes a durable record before and after; corrupt or truncated journal is detected, not trusted |
| ACT-7 | Undo / recovery | Supported operations restore exactly; undo previews drift and refuses to overwrite newer user changes; unsupported undo is stated, never implied |
| ACT-8 | Cancellation | Mid-run cancel leaves a consistent, reported partial state with recovery available |
| ACT-9 | Crash recovery | Kill the process mid-run; on restart the interrupted operation is detected and offered recovery |
| ACT-10 | Partial failure | Per-item failures are itemized; successful items are not rolled back silently and failed items are not reported as done |

### 35.4 Class C — privileged or irreversible action (all ACT tests plus)

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| PRV-1 | Protected targets | Protected resources (core services, system files, boot entries, security software) are refused by a typed rule that cannot be overridden from the renderer |
| PRV-2 | Never auto-elevate | KNOuX never raises itself; an elevated step happens only through an OS-provided elevation prompt that the user answers, and a denied prompt yields `needs-elevation`, not a retry loop |
| PRV-3 | Irreversibility disclosure | Irreversible effects are named before approval; no "undo" is offered or implied where none exists |
| PRV-4 | Simulated runner | The executor is exercised against a simulated runner (mock SCM, mock `winget`, mock `dism`/`sfc`, test registry hive, test profile) with exact-argument assertions |
| PRV-5 | Real-effect gate | A real-system execution requires disposable resources or an isolated Windows VM plus explicit operation-specific consent recorded in the evidence; otherwise the evidence says `NOT RUN` |
| PRV-6 | No shell | Execution uses `shell: false` with an argument array built in Main from validated fields; renderer strings are never concatenated into a command |
| PRV-7 | Effect verification | The effect is verified by re-reading system state, not by trusting an exit code |
| PRV-8 | Exit-code mapping | Each documented and observed non-zero exit maps to a typed error with an actionable message |

### 35.5 Class D — external / connected

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| EXT-1 | No-credential state | With no client ID, key or token configured, the control is disabled with the exact missing item named and the item appears in the handover; nothing is faked |
| EXT-2 | OAuth flow (where OAuth applies) | System-browser authorization-code flow with PKCE and loopback redirect; `state` checked; no embedded webview; tested against a local mock authorization server labeled as a test double |
| EXT-3 | Token custody | Tokens live only in Main, encrypted with OS-backed storage; absent from renderer, logs, exports and backups; verified by scanning all three |
| EXT-4 | Scope minimality | The requested scope set equals the declared minimal set for the exposed features; adding a feature that needs a broader scope requires a new consent |
| EXT-5 | Revocation | Disconnect deletes local tokens and calls the provider's revocation endpoint; a revocation failure is reported and local deletion still happens |
| EXT-6 | Quota / rate limit / backoff | 429 and quota errors back off with bounded retry; idempotent requests only are retried |
| EXT-7 | Offline and provider error | Offline, 401, 403, 5xx each produce a distinct honest state |
| EXT-8 | Data minimization | Only fields the feature displays are requested and stored; stored external data has a retention rule and a purge action |
| EXT-9 | Outbound allowlist | Main contacts only the declared provider hosts; any other destination is refused by test |

### 35.6 Specialized suites referenced by name

| Suite | Used by | Defined in |
|---|---|---|
| WG-1 … WG-14 | D05.08, D05.10 (and D05.01 / D05.06 where they consume WinGet data) | Section 36.8 |
| GOOG-1 … GOOG-12 | D04.01–D04.10, D14.03 | Section 37.8 |
| SENS-1 … SENS-14 | D10.26, D10.30, D10.31, D07.09, D10.32, D13.09 (battery gating consumer) | Section 38.7 |
| PRB-1 … PRB-6 | D10.16, D10.18, D10.19 (active probes) | Volume 4 Section 23.1 rules, restated in the matrix |
| SCM-1 … SCM-6 | D12.02, D12.03, D12.11 | Section 35.7 |
| REP-1 … REP-6 | D11.03, D11.05, D11.07, D11.08, D11.12, D10.24 | Section 35.7 |
| HND-1 … HND-3 | Hand-off surfaces whose ledger class is B/C but whose own deliverable is observation plus a link into the owning domain (D01.01–D01.06, D01.08, D01.12, D02.03, D02.05, D02.10) | Section 35.7 |

### 35.7 Definitions of the specialized suites not given elsewhere

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| PRB-1 | Target validation | Hostname/IP syntax, length limit and metacharacter rejection happen in Main; an invalid target never reaches a probe |
| PRB-2 | Allowlisted probe set | Only the declared probe types run; adding one requires a code change and a new consent text |
| PRB-3 | Bounds | Count, rate and total duration are bounded and the bounds are shown to the user before the first send |
| PRB-4 | First-use consent | The first use of each probe type shows what will be sent and to whom; consent is stored and revocable |
| PRB-5 | Cancellation | Cancel stops sending immediately; the child process tree is gone |
| PRB-6 | Local-sweep limits | Any LAN sweep is limited to the user's own subnet, size-capped, rate-limited and off by default |
| SCM-1 | Mock SCM transitions | Stop/start/recovery against a mock Service Control Manager reproduce the documented state machine |
| SCM-2 | Protected set | The seven protected-service cases recorded by the ledger remain refused; adding a service to the protected set needs a typed change, not a renderer flag |
| SCM-3 | Permission denial | An access-denied SCM call yields `needs-elevation` with no retry loop and no auto-elevation |
| SCM-4 | Stale / expired / unapproved plans | Each is refused with a distinct error |
| SCM-5 | Interrupted transition recovery | A transition interrupted by process death is detected on restart and reconciled against real service state |
| SCM-6 | Receipts | Receipts are written to the shared DPAPI-protected store; tampering is detected; the store is shared with Startup without regression |
| REP-1 | Simulated runner argv | The runner is invoked with the exact fixed argument array and no renderer-derived text |
| REP-2 | Output parsing | Recorded real tool output (including a non-English OS) parses to typed progress and results; unrecognized output is an error, not success |
| REP-3 | Restore point / backup precondition | Where a restore point or backup is a stated precondition, absence blocks execution with a reason |
| REP-4 | Cancel during run | Cancel is acknowledged, the tool's process tree is killed, and the post-cancel system state is reported as unknown-until-reread |
| REP-5 | Reboot-required | A pending-reboot outcome is reported as such and never as completed |
| REP-6 | Journal | The audit journal records intent, consent, argv class, exit code, duration and re-read result |
| HND-1 | No mutation from this surface | An IPC spy across the whole interaction shows no mutating channel is called from the hand-off surface; its controls navigate or start read-only work only |
| HND-2 | Context hand-off | The deep link opens the owning service at the right stage with the measured context (source, session, plan reference) and no approval token |
| HND-3 | Honest empty and stale | With no source, no session or a stale plan, the surface shows the empty or stale state with its reason; no number appears without a measurement |

## 36. WinGet — D05.08 Software Update Center and D05.10 Package Center & WinGet Browser

### 36.1 Identity and reconciliation

| Item | D05.08 | D05.10 |
|---|---|---|
| Catalog ID | `app-updates` | `app-store-packages` |
| Name | Software Update Center | Package Center & WinGet Browser |
| Ledger class | C (privileged/irreversible, eventual) | C (eventual) |
| Registry (2026-10-26) | **PARTIAL** — action provider "absent (gated)"; proof "WinGet parse fixture"; missing "vendor/WinGet executor + rollback plan" | **MEASURED_READ_ONLY** — class A; proof "test:apps controlled registry"; missing "none" |
| Acceptance doc | "Check bounded WinGet update feed; no install"; install/update "blocked to Mission 11" | "Read installed/Appx package rows and WinGet repository sample"; "no install or source agreement" |
| Current-slice assessment | Not reassessed | Not reassessed |
| **Reconciled status** | **Partially implemented** — read-only discovery exists; no execution engine established | **Read-only by intended design** — discovery/search exists; classed C only because a future install action would be C |

The ledger class C for D05.10 describes the *eventual* family of actions. The **current deliverable of D05.10 is observation**. Installing from search results is a separate action that this volume assigns to the D05.08 executor (36.5) so that there is one install/upgrade engine, one consent model and one journal, not two.

### 36.2 Confirmed existing implementation (DIRECTIVE)

The native application provider is `apps/gemini-ui/electron/native/app-bridge.cjs`. Relevant functions: `detectWinget()`, `updates()`, `packages()`, `parseWingetTable()`.

| Operation | Existing behavior |
|---|---|
| D05.08 `updates()` | Invokes the WinGet CLI for **read-only upgrade discovery**: `winget.exe upgrade --include-unknown --disable-interactivity` |
| D05.10 `packages()` | Invokes the WinGet CLI for **package discovery**: `winget.exe search <validated-query> --disable-interactivity` |
| Installed AppX/MSIX packages | Use the local application inventory (not WinGet) |
| `detectWinget()` | Detects whether the CLI is available |
| `parseWingetTable()` | Parses the CLI's text table into rows |
| Source agreements | **Not accepted automatically.** The existing calls do not pass `--accept-source-agreements` |

**Rule for Codex and for every report:** these two services must not be described as having an installation or upgrade execution engine unless an executor is found in the actual source. The directive, the registry ("action provider: absent (gated)") and the acceptance document ("install/update blocked") all agree that none is established.

### 36.3 Interface determination (resolved without owner input)

**Decision.** The WinGet **CLI with table parsing** is the verified current interface and remains the required discovery path. It is not replaced by the COM API or the PowerShell module on speculation.

**Basis.**
- DIRECTIVE: the implementation uses the CLI today.
- OFFICIAL: Microsoft's `winget search` page documents the options `--source`, `--count` (1–1000), `--exact`, `--accept-source-agreements` ("Accept all source agreements during source operations") and `--disable-interactivity`, and documents **no output-format option**. The default query is a case-insensitive substring match on name, ID and moniker. (The `winget upgrade` page was not retrievable in this session; the `--include-unknown` behavior below is DIRECTIVE, not re-read.)
- KNOWLEDGE (confirm on the owner host): WinGet writes localized, width-sensitive text tables, so the parser must key on **structure**, not on English header words, and must not trust truncated cells.

**Consequence.** `parseWingetTable()` is a safety-critical function. It needs a recorded-output fixture corpus (36.8, WG-2) and an explicit `parse-unrecognized-format` failure state. A table that does not parse is an error with the raw text length and exit code recorded, never an empty list presented as "no updates".

**Optional structured path.** If the owner host exposes the WinGet COM API or the Microsoft.WinGet.Client PowerShell module, a capability probe may record it (`winget.structuredInterface: available|absent`). Using it is permitted only after it has its own fixtures and returns results equal to the CLI path on the same host. Absence is normal and not an error.

### 36.4 Verified gaps (D05.08 and D05.10)

| # | Gap class | Gap | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | action executor | No install/upgrade executor is established for D05.08 | registry, acceptance doc |
| G2 | consent boundary | Source-agreement handling is undefined: with `--disable-interactivity` WinGet cannot prompt, and the code does not pass the accept flag, so a first-run host will fail or return nothing useful | DIRECTIVE |
| G3 | error handling | Behavior for missing `winget.exe` (App Installer absent), timeout, non-zero exit and unparseable output is not established | NOT VERIFIED |
| G4 | automated test | Only a "WinGet parse fixture" is recorded; no corpus covering localization, truncation, unknown versions or pinned packages | registry |
| G5 | real Windows runtime proof | No recorded run of the real CLI on the owner host in the ledger | ledger silent |
| G6 | cancellation | Whether cancelling kills the WinGet process tree is NOT VERIFIED (the implementation CSV records typed cancellation acknowledgement for system services; no such record exists for the Applications domain) | NOT VERIFIED |
| G7 | undo/recovery | No rollback plan exists ("vendor/WinGet executor + rollback plan" is listed as missing). WinGet has no general rollback; this is a documented platform limitation, not something to simulate | registry |
| G8 | integration | D05.10 install-from-search has no destination; D05.08 execution has none | acceptance doc |

### 36.5 Required behavior

**A. Read-only discovery (applies now, no consent beyond the user opening the service).**

1. `detectWinget()` returns a typed capability record: `{ state: available | provider-missing | blocked, version?, path?, sources[]?, probedAt }`. Missing App Installer is `provider-missing` with a reason, not an error.
2. Calls use `shell: false` and an argument array. The search query is validated in Main (length limit, no control characters, no leading `-`, passed as a single argument after `--query` semantics are confirmed on the host) and is never interpolated into a command string.
3. Discovery output is bounded (time, bytes, rows). A bound hit is reported ("showing first N").
4. **Source-agreement state machine.** If WinGet reports that a source agreement must be accepted, the provider returns `source-agreement-required` with the source name. The UI shows what will be accepted and which source it applies to. Only after an explicit user action is the call repeated with `--accept-source-agreements` **for that source and invocation**. The acceptance is recorded (source, time, WinGet version) and can be revoked by clearing the record. KNOuX never passes the flag silently, never passes it by default, and never stores a blanket acceptance unless the user explicitly chooses "remember for this source" (a product-policy default recorded in 42.2).
5. Rows carry: id, name, installed version, available version, source, and a `versionKnown` flag. `--include-unknown` rows with no known installed version are labeled "installed version unknown" and are excluded from any automatic action.
6. Result provenance: command class, WinGet version, exit code, duration, row count, parse status.
7. No row is invented. A package that WinGet lists but KNOuX cannot match to local inventory is shown unmatched.

**B. Upgrade/install executor (a new engine; owned by D05.08; D05.10 hands off to it).**

Service-class C. The following is REQUIRED before any statement that D05.08 "updates applications":

1. **Plan.** A plan lists exact package IDs, source, current version, target version, scope (user/machine if known), whether elevation is likely, and the exact fixed argument class. The plan has an immutable hash.
2. **Identity allowlist.** The package ID executed must equal an ID returned by the most recent discovery in this session and re-confirmed immediately before execution by an exact-ID lookup (`--exact`). Table cells may be truncated, so an ID taken from a truncated cell is refused. A renderer-supplied ID not in the last discovery set is rejected.
3. **Consent.** Per-run, per-plan approval inside the app, naming each package and stating that WinGet runs the vendor's installer, that some installers close running programs, and that **KNOuX cannot roll an upgrade back**. Package-agreement acceptance (`--accept-package-agreements`) is part of that consent text and is passed only after approval.
4. **Execution.** Main spawns `winget.exe` with `shell: false`, an argument array assembled from validated fields (for example `upgrade --id <id> --exact --source <source> --silent --disable-interactivity` plus the consented accept flags), one package at a time, with a hard timeout and output cap. Progress is the number of packages completed plus WinGet's own reported phase text when present; no invented percentage.
5. **Elevation.** KNOuX does not elevate itself. If a package needs administrator rights, the result is `needs-elevation` with the OS prompt behavior WinGet/the installer produces; a declined or absent prompt is reported, never retried silently.
6. **Cancellation.** Cancel kills the process tree, then reports the package as `cancelled-state-unknown` and re-reads inventory to establish the truth. It never claims the previous version is intact.
7. **Verification.** After each package, re-inventory (local registry/AppX and an exact `list`-style lookup) and compare the installed version with the plan's target. Only a verified match is `updated`. A mismatch with exit code 0 is `unverified-mismatch`.
8. **Journal.** Intent, consent time, plan hash, argv class, exit code, duration, before/after versions and the verification result are written to the shared DPAPI-protected receipt store used by the other native engines. There is **no undo**; the receipt offers "open the vendor/WinGet page" and "reinstall previous version if the source offers it" only as manual guidance, labeled as such.
9. **Crash recovery.** If the app dies mid-run, the next start finds the open journal entry, re-reads inventory and presents `interrupted` with the observed state.
10. **Protected targets.** An allowlist-independent blocklist refuses updating KNOuX itself while running, security software, and any package the user has excluded.

**C. D05.10 specifics.** Search is observation. "Install" from a search result creates a D05.08 **plan** (never a direct run). AppX/MSIX rows come from local inventory and are labeled as such; WinGet's `msstore` source behavior is probed, not assumed.

### 36.6 Error taxonomy (REQUIRED)

| Code | Meaning | UI behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `provider-missing` | `winget.exe` not found / App Installer absent | Disabled with reason; no retry loop |
| `source-agreement-required` | A source needs agreement acceptance | Show the consent step (36.5.A.4) |
| `source-unavailable` | A source cannot be reached (offline, DNS, certificate, source reset needed) | Show which source; offer retry; never show "no updates" |
| `parse-unrecognized-format` | Output did not match a known table structure | Error with exit code and byte count; offer "copy diagnostic" with redaction |
| `timeout` | Hard timeout hit | Process tree killed; partial rows discarded unless explicitly marked partial |
| `needs-elevation` | Installer/OS requires administrator | Per 36.5.B.5 |
| `package-not-found` | Exact ID no longer resolves | Re-run discovery |
| `installer-failed` | Non-zero exit from WinGet or installer | Show exit code mapped from observed codes; link to journal |
| `reboot-required` | Outcome indicates a restart | Report as pending, never as complete |
| `cancelled-state-unknown` | User cancelled | Re-inventory and report observed state |
| `unverified-mismatch` | Exit 0 but version not as planned | Report as unverified |

The numeric WinGet exit codes are **not** listed here because they were not re-read from an official page in this session. Codex records the observed code for each condition on the owner host and in the fixture corpus (WG-2, WG-9), and cites the official WinGet return-code reference in the evidence record.

### 36.7 Security boundaries

- No arbitrary shell or generic exec endpoint; the renderer cannot supply a program, flag or argument beyond the validated fields above.
- Query and ID validation in Main, not in the renderer.
- WinGet runs as the current user; machine-scope installs rely on the OS elevation behavior, not on KNOuX.
- Source agreements and package agreements are accepted only after visible, recorded user consent.
- Output shown to the user is escaped; raw installer output is size-capped and redacted for user paths and account names on export.

### 36.8 Acceptance tests — suite WG

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| WG-1 | Capability detection | Fixtures for present, absent and blocked `winget.exe` yield the correct typed state; absence never throws |
| WG-2 | Parse corpus | Recorded real outputs for `upgrade` and `search` — English, Arabic (RTL) OS language, narrow-console truncation, wide characters, unknown installed versions, pinned packages, an empty result, a result with a footer line — parse to the exact expected rows; each unrecognized input yields `parse-unrecognized-format` |
| WG-3 | Truncated ID | A row whose ID cell is truncated is not executable and is shown with a "confirm package" step |
| WG-4 | Argument safety | Query strings containing quotes, `;`, `&`, `|`, `%`, backticks, a leading `-` and 5,000 characters are either rejected or passed as one inert argument; a spy runner proves the exact argv |
| WG-5 | Source-agreement flow | A mock `winget` that demands agreement → `source-agreement-required`; before consent the accept flag is absent from argv; after consent it is present once; the consent record exists; revoking clears it |
| WG-6 | Offline / source failure | A mock source failure yields `source-unavailable`, never an empty "up to date" |
| WG-7 | Bounds and timeout | A mock that hangs or floods output is killed at the bound and the tree is gone; partial data is labeled |
| WG-8 | Plan binding | An upgrade plan executes only with its hash; changing one package invalidates approval; a stale discovery is refused |
| WG-9 | Mock executor | A mock `winget` for upgrade verifies exact argv, one package at a time, the accept flags only after consent, timeout, and each mapped exit code |
| WG-10 | Verification | After a mock upgrade the inventory re-read decides `updated` vs `unverified-mismatch` |
| WG-11 | Cancel and crash | Cancel kills the process tree and reports `cancelled-state-unknown`; a kill mid-run is recovered as `interrupted` on restart |
| WG-12 | Elevation | A mock needing administrator yields `needs-elevation` and never loops or self-elevates |
| WG-13 | Real-host read-only | On the owner Windows host, record `winget --info` output and one real `upgrade` and `search` run (read-only), store them as new fixtures with the WinGet version, and show the parser output equals the console output row-for-row. Labeled REAL-HOST |
| WG-14 | No dead control | Every control in D05.08/D05.10 either works or is disabled with the true reason; no "Update all" appears until WG-8–WG-12 pass |

### 36.9 Evidence required for completion

- D05.10 is complete as an observation service when WG-1…WG-7, WG-13, WG-14 and U-1…U-7 pass and the evidence record names the WinGet version and OS language of the real-host run.
- D05.08 is complete as an **update center** only when, in addition, WG-8…WG-12 pass with the mock executor **and** one disposable-package real execution has been run with explicit recorded owner consent (or the evidence states `NOT RUN — no owner consent for a real installation` and the status stays Partially implemented).
- A real installation of any package on the owner's machine is a destructive/irreversible action under Volume 1 Section 10.5 and requires operation-specific consent at the time of use.

## 37. Google Workspace — OAuth Scopes, Classification and Verification (D04.01–D04.10, D14.03)

### 37.1 What the archive establishes about requested scopes

The directive asked Codex's author to "determine the actual requested Gmail and People API scopes from the project". The archive answers this at ledger level, and the answer matters:

| Fact | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **D04.01 Google Sheets** has a built **read-only** provider: main-process OAuth **PKCE**, tokens protected by Electron `safeStorage` (DPAPI on Windows), read-only Sheets API v4, actions Connect / add spreadsheet URL or ID / refresh metadata / read bounded A1 range / disconnect. | `SERVICE_CAPABILITY_MATRIX.md` row mission-04-01; `SERVICE_WORKBENCH_ACCEPTANCE.md` |
| It requests **"four fixed scopes"**. The scope strings are **not in the archive**. | same rows ("Explicit browser OAuth consent; four fixed scopes; no access/refresh token in renderer") |
| Verification recorded: `test:google` **14 checks**; dev/packaged smoke `googleOk` in the **disconnected** state; **live proof NOT PERFORMED**. | same rows |
| The Workspace hub UI has a **"10-scope UI test"** (a local consent *review*, not a live request). | `SERVICE_REGISTRY_EVIDENCE.md` rows `google-*` |
| **D04.02 Gmail, D04.03 Tasks, D04.04 Forms, D04.05 Contacts, D04.06 Calendar, D04.07 Picker, D04.08 Slides, D04.09 Chat, D04.10 Classroom** are "fixture preview only; **live provider and scope not implemented**". | ledger baseline; matrix rows mission-04-02…10 |
| **D14.03 Google Workspace Hub** is `BLOCKED_REQUIRES_USER_ACTION — no OAuth connection`; no Google account, token or API request exists. | ledger; registry `settings-workspace` |
| The implementation CSV for this slice states "no scopes/provider changes" for D04.01. | `SERVICE_WORKSPACE_IMPLEMENTATION.csv` |

**Therefore:** today **no Gmail scope and no People/Contacts scope is requested by any live code path.** The earlier concern that "Gmail and Contacts integrations require verification" concerns a *future* scope choice, and the answer differs sharply by scope. The one concrete unknown that Codex must close first is the exact list of the four Sheets scopes, because an extra Drive scope would change the classification of the whole consent.

### 37.2 First action for Codex (read-only, no owner input)

1. Search the whole tree for scope strings: every occurrence of `googleapis.com/auth` and `openid`, `email`, `profile`; list file, line, constant name, and which service uses it.
2. Record the four Sheets scopes and the ten hub scopes in a table with columns *scope string · service · classification · source of classification · date checked*. Commit it as a test fixture (GOOG-1).
3. For each scope, read its category from the Google Cloud Console scope table or Google's published scope list. OFFICIAL: the Console shows each scope's category automatically when scopes are added to a project. Do not rely on this volume's KNOWLEDGE-tagged classifications without that check.
4. If any scope is **restricted** (for example a full Drive read scope) and the Sheets feature does not need it, remove it as a minimality fix (EXT-4). This is a technical correction, not a product decision.

### 37.3 Official classification and verification model

**OFFICIAL — Google Cloud Help, "OAuth app verification" (https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/13463073):**

| Category | Verification requirement as stated |
|---|---|
| Non-sensitive | Verification is optional ("not mandatory"). Brand verification is a lighter process needed only to show the app name and logo on the consent screen. |
| Sensitive | The app must complete **OAuth app verification** before access is granted (to the general public). |
| Restricted | Same verification, plus **annual re-verification**; the page names a **security assessment** for restricted-scope apps and links to a separate page for its details. |
| Categories | Appear automatically in the Console when scopes are added to the project. |

**OFFICIAL — Gmail API scopes (https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/api/auth/scopes), read this session:**

| Class | Scopes |
|---|---|
| Non-sensitive | `gmail.addons.current.action.compose`, `gmail.addons.current.message.action`, `gmail.labels` |
| Sensitive | `gmail.addons.current.message.metadata`, `gmail.addons.current.message.readonly`, `gmail.send` |
| **Restricted** | `https://mail.google.com/`, `gmail.readonly`, `gmail.compose`, `gmail.insert`, `gmail.modify`, `gmail.metadata`, `gmail.settings.basic`, `gmail.settings.sharing` |

Consequences for D04.02: any feature that **reads message content or metadata** (`gmail.readonly`, even `gmail.metadata`) uses a **restricted** scope. Sending only (`gmail.send`) is sensitive. Label management only (`gmail.labels`) is non-sensitive but exposes no message content. A mailbox *viewer* cannot be built on non-sensitive scopes.

**OFFICIAL — People API scopes (https://developers.google.com/contacts), read this session:** `contacts` (read/write personal contacts and groups; legacy `m8/feeds` alias), `contacts.readonly`, `contacts.other.readonly` ("Other Contacts": names, emails and phone numbers only), `directory.readonly` (Global Address List endpoints). **That page states no sensitivity classification.** KNOWLEDGE (confirm in the Console): the `contacts*` and `directory.readonly` scopes are classified **sensitive**, not restricted. Treat that as unconfirmed until step 3 of 37.2.

**KNOWLEDGE (not re-read this session; confirm in the Console and Google's current policy pages before any release decision):**
- A project whose publishing status is **Testing** can authorize only listed **test users** (a small capped number), shows an "unverified app" warning, and — for external apps using non-basic scopes — issues **refresh tokens that expire after about seven days**, forcing re-authorization. This is the legitimate path for the owner's own use of any sensitive or restricted scope **without** verification.
- Google's security-assessment requirement for restricted scopes is tied to apps that access restricted-scope data **through servers**. Whether a purely local, client-side desktop app that never transmits data to a developer server is treated differently must be confirmed from the current "restricted scope verification" page. Do not assume an exemption; do not assume an assessment is required. Record the page, date and wording in the evidence.

### 37.4 Distinctions Codex must preserve in the product and in reports

| Situation | What it means | What the product does |
|---|---|---|
| **Non-sensitive scopes only** | No Google verification required to serve any user | Normal connect flow |
| **Sensitive scope, owner-only use** | Works in Testing status with the owner as test user; unverified-app screen; refresh token may expire in days | `reauthorization-required` state (not a generic error); handover lists "OAuth client + test user" |
| **Sensitive scope, public release** | OAuth app verification required before ordinary accounts can grant it | Release-blocking item in the handover; local code still complete |
| **Restricted scope, owner-only use** | Same Testing path; same warnings | Same |
| **Restricted scope, public release** | Verification, annual re-verification, and a possible security assessment (37.3 caveat) | Treated as an owner product-policy decision (42.2); the connector stays behind a build/feature flag until decided |
| **Workspace-only data** (directory, some Chat/Classroom data) | May need a Workspace/Education account and admin settings | Capability probe reports `account-type-unsupported` honestly |

A statement that "Gmail and Contacts integrations all need the same verification" is **wrong** and must not appear in any report.

### 37.5 Per-service scope plan (technical defaults; owner policy items are in 42.2)

Volume 5 (Section 26.2) specifies, for several services, a read behavior **and** an optional write behavior. The two have different scopes, and the rule in Volume 5 Section 26.1 applies: request the narrowest scope per feature, **incrementally at the moment of first use**. So each service below has a *read scope set* (requested when the user connects the service) and a *write scope set* (requested only if and when the user first uses a write feature, with its own consent screen).

Classification column: **O** = OFFICIAL this session, **K** = KNOWLEDGE to confirm in the Console, **?** = not established; read the category from the Console before coding.

| Ref | Service | Read scope set | Class | Write scope set named in Volume 5 | Class | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D04.01 | Sheets | `spreadsheets.readonly` (+ `openid`/`email` if the account is shown) | sensitive **K** | `spreadsheets`, or `drive.file` for files created or opened through Picker | sensitive **K** / non-sensitive **K** | **Existing provider is read-only** (four scopes, strings to be recorded). Volume 5's *export-to-sheet* write feature is **not built** (gap G-D04.01-W). Using `drive.file` + Picker for writes avoids a sensitive scope |
| D04.02 | Gmail | `gmail.readonly` **or** `gmail.metadata` | **restricted O** (both) | `gmail.send` **or** `gmail.compose` | sensitive **O** / **restricted O** | Volume 5 lists `gmail.metadata` as if it were lighter; it is restricted too, so **no non-restricted path to a message list exists**. `gmail.labels` (non-sensitive **O**) exposes no message content. Send-only via `gmail.send` is sensitive. Not implemented today |
| D04.03 | Tasks | `tasks.readonly` | sensitive **K** | `tasks` | sensitive **K** | No Workspace requirement |
| D04.04 | Forms | `forms.body.readonly`, `forms.responses.readonly` | ? | `forms.body` | ? | Responses contain third-party personal data |
| D04.05 | Contacts | `contacts.readonly` (optional `contacts.other.readonly`) | sensitive **K** (official People page lists the scopes, states no class) | `contacts` | sensitive **K** | `directory.readonly` is Workspace-only. Bulk writes are not offered |
| D04.06 | Calendar | `calendar.readonly` | sensitive **K** | `calendar.events` | sensitive **K** | |
| D04.07 | Picker | `drive.file` (+ Picker API key and App ID) | non-sensitive **K** | — | — | Browser widget needing a developer key and an allowed origin; hosting inside Electron is a design item, see 37.6. The existing paste-URL/ID flow for D04.01 works without it |
| D04.08 | Slides | `presentations.readonly` (+ `drive.file`) | sensitive **K** / non-sensitive **K** | `presentations` | sensitive **K** | |
| D04.09 | Chat | Chat API user-authorized read scopes | ? | message-create scope | ? | Volume 5 states a Workspace account and an administrator-approved Chat app are required for most operations (KNOWLEDGE, unverified this session); consumer accounts report `unsupported-account-type` |
| D04.10 | Classroom | Classroom read scopes (courses, rosters, coursework) | ? | coursework write only if explicitly chosen | ? | Eligible Workspace-for-Education or supported Classroom account only; students' data is never exported or sent to AI by default |

### 37.6 Required architecture (applies to every D04 service and D14.03)

- **Hub/service split.** D14.03 owns connection state, the token vault, disconnect and revoke, and the "connected accounts" view. Each D04 service owns its API adapter, its scope declaration and its consent summary. One OAuth implementation, one vault.
- **Incremental, per-service consent.** Connecting Sheets requests only Sheets scopes; Gmail scopes are requested only when the user connects Gmail. The current "10-scope" hub review is a UI review, never a combined live request. Restricted scopes are never in a default preset.
- **System-browser desktop flow.** Authorization-code flow with PKCE and a loopback redirect, `state` verified, no embedded webview, executed in Main. Existing D04.01 behavior is preserved, not rewritten.
- **Token custody.** Tokens and client configuration are read only in Main. Encryption uses the OS-backed `safeStorage`. Tokens are excluded from renderer, logs, exports, the profile backup (D14.07) and any evidence file.
- **Revocation.** Disconnect deletes local tokens and calls Google's revocation endpoint; failure to reach Google is reported, local deletion still occurs.
- **Quota/backoff and bounds.** Reads are bounded (rows, pages, bytes), paged, rate-limited and cacheable with an explicit time-to-live; 429 and quota errors back off.
- **No fabricated state.** Without a configured client or consent, each service shows the disabled/"connect" state with the exact missing item; local fixtures remain labeled "sample preview" and are never presented as the user's data.
- **Credential names.** The settings key or environment variable that holds the OAuth client ID (and, if the client type uses one, the client secret) is **not in the archive**. Codex records the real names found in code in the handover (names only, never values).

### 37.7 Specific external blockers (replace the blanket "needs Google verification")

| ID | Blocker | Needed from | Affects | Local work that is still done |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B-G1 | A Google Cloud project with an OAuth client of the desktop/installed-app type, and the client ID configured in KNOuX settings (names per code) | Owner | D04.01 live proof; every later D04 service | All provider code, consent UI, mocks, tests |
| B-G2 | OAuth consent screen set up; owner added as a **test user** (Testing status) for any sensitive or restricted scope | Owner | Live use of D04.01–D04.10 by the owner | Same |
| B-G3 | **Live browser consent** by the owner for each service | Owner at time of use | Live proof of each service | Same |
| B-G4 | **OAuth app verification** (and annual re-verification) before ordinary users can grant sensitive/restricted scopes | Owner decision + Google | Public release of D04.02–D04.10 and of D04.01 if its scopes are sensitive | Everything else |
| B-G5 | **Security assessment**, if Google's current policy requires it for the chosen restricted scope and architecture (37.3 caveat) | Owner decision + assessor | Public release of a Gmail read feature | Everything else |
| B-G6 | Picker **API key and App ID**, if D04.07 is pursued | Owner | D04.07 live behavior | Local code behind the `needs-credential` state |
| B-G7 | Workspace/Education account for directory, and possibly Chat/Classroom data | Owner | D04.05 directory part, D04.09, D04.10 | Capability probe |

### 37.8 Acceptance tests — suite GOOG

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| GOOG-1 | Scope inventory | A test enumerates every Google scope string in the source and fails if one is not in the declared per-service map |
| GOOG-2 | Classification record | Each declared scope has a category, the page or Console view it came from and the date; a **restricted** scope in the map fails the build unless the service carries an explicit owner-decision flag |
| GOOG-3 | Token custody | A scan of renderer bundle, logs, exports, backups and evidence files finds no access token, refresh token or client secret |
| GOOG-4 | PKCE and state | Against a local mock authorization server (labeled as a test double): wrong `state` rejected, missing verifier rejected, valid flow stores tokens only in the vault |
| GOOG-5 | Incremental consent | Connecting D04.01 requests only its declared scopes; no Gmail, Contacts or Drive-wide scope appears in the request |
| GOOG-6 | Revocation | Disconnect removes tokens, calls the (mock) revocation endpoint, tolerates its failure, and leaves no cached account data when the user selects purge |
| GOOG-7 | Testing-mode expiry | An `invalid_grant` from a (mock) expired refresh token yields `reauthorization-required` with a plain explanation, not a generic failure |
| GOOG-8 | User cancel and denial | `access_denied` and a closed browser tab end in a clean `cancelled` state with the connect control available |
| GOOG-9 | Quota and offline | 429 backs off with bounded retry; offline, 401, 403, 5xx are distinct states |
| GOOG-10 | Bounds and minimization | Reads are bounded; only displayed fields are requested and stored; the retention rule and purge action work |
| GOOG-11 | Outbound allowlist | Main refuses any destination other than the declared Google hosts |
| GOOG-12 | No-credential state | With no client configured, every D04 control is disabled with the exact missing item named and the handover lists it; no fixture data is presented as live |

### 37.9 Evidence required for completion

- Each D04 service reaches **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL** in the ledger's own vocabulary (every locally executable layer done and honestly gated) when GOOG-1…GOOG-12 and U-1…U-7 pass with mocks.
- It reaches **COMPLETE_VERIFIED** only with a live, owner-consented run recorded in the evidence (command or UI capture, time, account type, scope list granted, result), after B-G1…B-G3.
- Public-release readiness additionally needs B-G4 (and B-G5 if required). These are listed in the final handover; the work does not stop for them.

## 38. Hardware Sensors — Battery, Wear, Thermal and Disk Reliability (D10.26, D10.30, D10.31, D07.09, D10.32)

### 38.1 Evidence and reconciled status

| Service | Ledger evidence | Reconciled status |
|---|---|---|
| D10.26 Battery Charge & Health Instrument (`hardware-battery`) | Registry: live provider "`Win32_Battery` where present"; status PARTIAL; missing "battery not exposed on this host" | Partially implemented (historical). The *provider* exists; the owner's host exposes no battery, so battery behavior has **not** been exercised on real battery hardware |
| D10.30 Battery Wear & Cycle Count Ledger (`hardware-cycles`) | No registry row of its own; implementation CSV: "Existing typed read-only native coordinator", UNASSESSED | Not verified |
| D10.31 Thermal Sensors & Temperature Board (`hardware-thermal`) | Registry: "CIM thermal where present"; PARTIAL; "thermal sensor unavailable/partial" | Partially implemented (historical) |
| D07.09 Disk Reliability & S.M.A.R.T. Health (`storage-smart`) | Implementation CSV: **UNSUPPORTED**; "StorageShell has no physical SMART provider; separate System hardware data is not silently substituted"; gap "SMART routing and device identity correlation not implemented in Storage service"; UI "honest support probe retained; no fabricated health scores" | Not implemented (honest unsupported probe in place) |
| D10.32 Physical Disk Drives & Bus Interface (`hardware-disks`) | Typed read-only System coordinator; UNASSESSED | Not verified |

Two real constraints follow and are carried into the handover: (1) the owner's host has no battery and partial thermal data, so battery and thermal behavior can only be proven with **recorded fixtures** until a laptop or a different host is available (blocker B-S1); (2) D07.09 deliberately does not borrow System data without proven device-identity correlation.

### 38.2 Universal sensor rules (REQUIRED; restate and tighten Volume 4 Section 23.1)

1. **Capability-driven.** Nothing is assumed. A *capability probe* runs first and returns, per measurement, a record `{ capability, provider, state, reason, probedAt }`. States: `available`, `partial`, `unsupported-device` (hardware or firmware does not expose it), `provider-missing` (class, counter or interface absent), `needs-elevation`, `denied`, `error`. The UI enables a view only for `available` or `partial` measurements.
2. **No fabricated telemetry.** There is no default, placeholder, interpolated, smoothed, averaged-across-gaps, synthetic-history or "typical value" anywhere, including fixtures shown to users. **Zero is never a placeholder for unknown.** An absent field renders "—" with its reason.
3. **Timestamp and provenance on every sample:** `{ metric, value, unit, sampleTime, timeSource: 'os-reported' | 'read-time', provider, source (class/property/counter/IOCTL), deviceId (stable local identifier, redacted on export), rawValue }`. `rawValue` keeps the provider's number before unit conversion so conversions are auditable.
4. **Fixed queries only.** Typed, allowlisted queries; no renderer-supplied text reaches a command or query; `shell: false`; bounded time and output; process tree killed on cancel.
5. **No third-party kernel drivers.** KNOuX does not load or install a signed/unsigned driver (for example a ring-0 hardware-monitor driver) to obtain more sensors. If the standard providers expose nothing, the honest state is shown. Whether to relax this is a product-policy decision (42.2); the default is **no**.
6. **No elevation by KNOuX.** A measurement that needs administrator rights reports `needs-elevation` with the reason. The UI may link to the OS-provided mechanism; KNOuX does not relaunch itself elevated for sensors.
7. **Stored history only from real samples.** The sample store (SP-11) keeps timestamped real samples; gaps (sleep, sampler off, device removed) stay gaps; the first line segment appears only after two real samples.
8. **Device identity.** Serial numbers and unique IDs are hidden by default, expandable on request, and excluded from exports by default.
9. **Virtual machines, desktops, docking and drivers.** A desktop without a battery, a VM without SMBIOS detail, a USB bridge that blocks SMART pass-through and a laptop whose firmware returns static ACPI values are normal conditions with named states, never errors.

### 38.3 Battery — D10.26 and D10.30

**Provider chain** (KNOWLEDGE for the class and property names; confirm on a battery host and record the observed behavior). Each link is used only if the previous one lacks the field:

| Measurement | Source 1 | Source 2 | Source 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Presence, AC/battery state, charge % | Win32 power status (system power API) | `Win32_Battery` (`BatteryStatus`, `EstimatedChargeRemaining`) | — |
| OS-reported runtime | `Win32_Battery.EstimatedRunTime` | system power API remaining time | — |
| Voltage, charge/discharge rate, remaining capacity | `root\wmi` `BatteryStatus` | — | — |
| Design capacity | `root\wmi` `BatteryStaticData` (`DesignedCapacity`) | battery report (`powercfg /batteryreport` XML) | battery IOCTL information |
| Full-charge capacity | `root\wmi` `BatteryFullChargedCapacity` | battery report | battery IOCTL information |
| Cycle count | `root\wmi` `BatteryCycleCount` | battery report | battery IOCTL information |

**Rules.**
- No battery (the system power API reports no system battery; `Win32_Battery` returns no instance) ⇒ `unsupported-device`: the services show "No battery detected on this device" and nothing else. D13.09 treats power state as **unknown** and pauses for owner review per the existing rule; it never assumes AC.
- **Sentinel values.** Documented "unknown" encodings (for example the unknown runtime value used by the power APIs, or the unknown charge value) are mapped to *unknown*, never displayed as numbers. Codex lists every sentinel it handles, with the source of each, in the provider's header comment and in SENS-3.
- **Runtime is never computed by KNOuX.** It is shown only if the OS reports it.
- **Wear** (D10.30) = `1 − fullChargeCapacity / designCapacity`, computed only when **both** values exist, are positive, and come from the same unit family; the inputs are shown beside the result. If full-charge exceeds design the ledger shows "reported capacity exceeds design" and still shows the inputs; it does not clamp.
- **Cycle count.** Absent when the firmware does not expose it. A reported `0` is shown as "reported 0 (new battery or not supported by firmware)" and never used in arithmetic or trends as if certain.
- `needs-elevation` for any `root\wmi` class that the host restricts is reported per class; the other measurements continue.

### 38.4 Thermal — D10.31

**Provider chain** (KNOWLEDGE; confirm on the owner host and record):

| Source | What it gives | Typical limits |
|---|---|---|
| Thermal Zone Information performance counters (including the CIM wrapper class for them) | Per-zone temperature (Kelvin; a higher-resolution tenths-of-Kelvin value where available) | Present on many systems without elevation; reflects ACPI zones, not CPU die |
| `root\wmi` `MSAcpi_ThermalZoneTemperature` | `CurrentTemperature` in tenths of Kelvin | Often requires elevation; some firmware reports a fixed placeholder |
| Storage reliability counters for physical disks | Disk temperature and maximum, where the drive reports them | May require elevation; absent on many USB bridges and virtual disks |
| Vendor GPU or CPU interfaces | Out of scope unless the vendor's own user-mode library is detected | Reported `provider-missing` otherwise |

**Rules.**
- Conversion: °C = Kelvin − 273.15; tenths-of-Kelvin are divided by 10 first. `rawValue` is retained.
- A zone is listed only if it returned a value. Zero sensors ⇒ the honest empty state "No standard thermal sensors exposed by this device".
- **Plausibility without invention.** A value outside −40…150 °C is shown as "implausible reading (raw: …)" and excluded from charts; KNOuX does not substitute another value. A zone that returns the identical raw value across many samples while other measurements change is annotated "unchanged across N samples" — a factual observation, not a verdict that the sensor is fake.
- Zone names are shown as reported; KNOuX does not relabel an ACPI zone as "CPU" unless the provider says so.
- The board shows the source per row, sample time, and age.

### 38.5 Disk reliability and S.M.A.R.T. — D07.09 and D10.32

**Required before D07.09 may show anything beyond "not supported":**

1. **Device identity correlation.** Chain: volume → partition → disk number → physical disk (identifier and serial from the storage subsystem) → reliability data. A reliability record is attached to a volume only when every link resolves uniquely. A mismatch, a spanned or software-RAID volume, a USB bridge, a VM disk or a Storage Space ⇒ `unsupported-device` with the reason. This closes the ledger gap "SMART routing and device identity correlation not implemented in Storage service" without silently substituting System data.
2. **Sources** (KNOWLEDGE; confirm per host): physical-disk health and operational status from the storage subsystem; storage reliability counters (temperature, wear, error totals, power-on hours where reported); legacy ATA failure-prediction status in `root\wmi` where the host exposes it; NVMe health information where a supported user-mode query is available. Several of these require elevation.
3. **Presentation.** Only provider-reported fields: health status, predicted-failure flag, reported counters with units. **No KNOuX-invented health score or percentage.** Vendor-specific raw attributes are shown raw, and are interpreted only through a table-driven, vendor-keyed, versioned mapping with a source citation; unknown attributes remain raw.
4. **D10.32** continues to list physical disks and bus type from the System coordinator and links to D07.09; D07.09 may link back but never copies System numbers into Storage views without the correlation in step 1.

### 38.6 Consumers

D01.07 System Live Pulse, D02.09 System Health Pulse, D13.09 Power-Aware Scheduler and D10.36 Hardware Evidence Report read the **same capability records and samples**. None may show a battery or thermal value that the capability record marks unavailable.

### 38.7 Acceptance tests — suite SENS

| ID | Test | Pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| SENS-1 | Capability probe | Fixture hosts (desktop without battery, laptop, VM, restricted user) produce the exact capability records per measurement |
| SENS-2 | No battery | Desktop fixture ⇒ "No battery detected"; wear, cycles and runtime views are absent; D13.09 reports power state unknown and pauses |
| SENS-3 | Sentinels | Every listed sentinel value maps to unknown; no sentinel ever appears as a number or in a chart |
| SENS-4 | Battery profiles | Charging, discharging, AC-full, unknown runtime and absent profiles render exactly; runtime appears only when reported |
| SENS-5 | Wear arithmetic | Injected capacities yield the exact ratio with inputs displayed; missing, zero or inconsistent inputs yield no ratio; design < full-charge shows the stated inconsistency |
| SENS-6 | Cycle count | Missing ⇒ unavailable; reported 0 ⇒ the ambiguous label; no history appears before real readings |
| SENS-7 | Thermal conversion | Kelvin and tenths-of-Kelvin fixtures convert exactly; `rawValue` retained |
| SENS-8 | Thermal empty and partial | Zero zones ⇒ honest empty state; some zones denied ⇒ the rest render and the denied ones show `needs-elevation` |
| SENS-9 | Implausible and static | Out-of-range values are excluded from charts and labeled; unchanged-across-samples is annotated without a verdict |
| SENS-10 | No synthetic data | A repository-wide scan finds no hard-coded temperature, charge or health value in provider or UI code paths that a user can see as a measurement; fixtures are clearly under test directories and labeled |
| SENS-11 | Elevation | A provider denied for lack of administrator rights reports `needs-elevation`; KNOuX does not relaunch itself and does not loop |
| SENS-12 | Disk correlation | Fixtures for a unique internal disk, a USB bridge, a VM disk, a spanned volume and a mismatch show reliability data only for the first; others show `unsupported-device` with the reason |
| SENS-13 | No health score | No UI string or value in D07.09 is a KNOuX-computed score; provider fields only |
| SENS-14 | Real-host record | On the owner host, record the capability probe and the sensor outputs (including honest absences) as fixtures labeled REAL-HOST; on a battery host (if available) record battery fixtures labeled REAL-HOST; otherwise the evidence says `NOT RUN — no battery host` and battery status stays Partially implemented |

### 38.8 Evidence required for completion

- D10.26 / D10.30 / D10.31 / D07.09 reach **Implemented and fixture tested** when SENS-1…SENS-13 and U-1…U-7 pass.
- Moving D10.26/D10.30 above fixture level requires SENS-14 on real battery hardware (blocker B-S1). Moving D07.09 above fixture level requires a host whose storage exposes reliability data and, for elevation-dependent fields, an owner-run elevated read (B-S2).
- An honest "unsupported on this device" outcome is a **complete** result for an observation service when the capability probe proves it, and is recorded as such.

## 39. Execution Order (dependency order only — no schedule)

Dependency, not a calendar, decides sequence. Codex proceeds to the next independently safe item after each verified slice and never stops on a blocked domain.

1. **Observe and preserve.** Run the read-only inspections of Volume 1 Section 3.3 on the real repository; record output verbatim; verify the SHAs of Section 32.4 for reachability; confirm the external checkpoint and the donor branch are intact; create nothing destructive. Work in a separate git worktree; never reset, clean, force-push or delete.
2. **Reconcile the registry.** Open `native-contracts.ts`, the installed UI service registry and the mounted workspaces; fill the *catalog ID*, *mounted workspace* and *operation ID* columns of Section 40; resolve the 18 proposed aliases of 33.2 and give the 31 D01/D02/D03 rows registry IDs or record why none exists.
3. **Shared services first.** Capability registry, operation coordinator, event bus, journal/receipt store (shared DPAPI-protected store already used by Startup and Services), authorization/scope manager, filesystem walker, process runner (with process-tree kill), sample store (SP-11). Extend, do not duplicate.
4. **Resolved determinations.** Close the explicit gaps of Sections 36–38 that unblock many rows: WinGet parser corpus and agreement state machine; Google scope inventory and classification test; sensor capability probe.
5. **Domain 03, then Smart Scan, Files, Storage, Cleanup, Repair, Startup, Services, System, Applications, Automation, Settings/AI, Google Workspace, Home** (Volume 1 Section 14.11), each by the per-service blocks of Section 41. Hand-off services (HND) follow their owning domain.
6. **After every slice:** run the tests of Section 35 for the touched rows on the exact SHA, record the evidence (Volume 1 Section 10.2), update the LIVE ledger and implementation CSV, and commit only verified, owned changes. No automatic merge to protected `main`; no merge of donor or OpenHands branches without reviewed diffs (Volume 1 Section 11).
7. **Preserve the visual identity.** Living Glass headers, transparent artwork, orbital engine, compact selectors and existing workspaces are extended, never replaced. The visual stream remains Codex's and owner approval (gate 10) remains open; this volume adds no visual requirement.

## 41. Per-Service Evidence Addenda and Acceptance Tests (all 181 entries)

Each block restates, for one service, the facts from the evidence archive and binds the exact acceptance tests. The **service-specific acceptance** line is copied verbatim from the contract in Volumes 1–5 (the contracts themselves are unchanged and still hold the objective, backend, frontend, lifecycle, safety and error detail). The lifecycle, security and error lines are the class defaults of this volume; the per-service contract adds domain detail and wins on conflict if stricter.

### 41.0 Class defaults referenced by every block

| Code | Text |
|---|---|
| L-A | Discover → Configure (authorized scope or fixed query) → Observe (measured) → Record (sample/receipt, bounded) → Export. No effect on files, registry, services or network. |
| L-B | Discover → Configure → Preview (exact plan, conflicts, risk) → Authorize (plan-hash-bound, per-operation consent) → Execute (bounded, real progress) → Verify (postconditions, before/after manifest) → Record (durable journal) → Recover (undo/restore where the platform allows). |
| L-C | Discover → Preview → Authorize (explicit per-action consent; OS elevation prompt only if the OS demands it) → Execute through a supported OS/vendor mechanism with a fixed argument array → Verify by re-reading system state → Record → Recover only where the platform supports it; otherwise disclosed as irreversible. |
| L-D | Not connected → needs-credential → needs-consent → Connect (system browser, PKCE) → Read (bounded) → [Write: preview → operation-specific consent → verify by read-back] → Disconnect (revoke, purge). |
| S-A | Read-only. Authorized scope or allowlisted typed query; Main re-validates every path and argument; sensitive fields redacted on export; reports evidence, never a safety or malware verdict. |
| S-B | Class B: reversible action behind plan-bound consent; renderer untrusted; targets re-authorized in Main; no overwrite; quarantine/journaled move before any removal; no claim of freed space beyond what is physically freed. |
| S-C | Class C: protected-target blocklist, no auto-elevation, `shell:false` fixed argv, irreversibility disclosed, per-action consent, real execution only on disposable resources or with recorded owner consent. |
| S-D | Class D: credentials only in Main (OS-protected store); least-privilege scopes requested incrementally; explicit consent before every connection and every write; outbound allowlist; data minimization and purge. |
| E-A | Provider-missing, access-denied, needs-elevation, timeout and malformed output are distinct typed states. Cancel stops the walker/query and kills the child-process tree. Restart shows the last real sample with its age. No placeholder fills a gap. |
| E-B | Stale plan rejected; per-item failures itemized; cancel leaves a consistent reported partial state; crash on restart is detected from the journal and offered recovery; undo previews drift and never overwrites newer user changes. |
| E-C | Denied elevation → `needs-elevation` without retry loop; non-zero exit mapped to a typed error; cancel kills the process tree and reports the system state as unknown until re-read; reboot-required reported as pending; interrupted runs reconciled against real state on restart. |
| E-D | Offline, 401, 403, 429, 5xx and `invalid_grant` are distinct states; refresh failure → needs-reauthorization; 429 backs off boundedly; cancel aborts in-flight requests; disconnect always deletes local tokens even if revocation fails. |
| V-A | Evidence record (§10.2) for OBS-1…OBS-7 on fixtures **and** at least one real Windows host run labeled REAL-HOST; UI matrix captures (U-5); ledger row updated with SHA. An honest "unsupported here" outcome proven by the capability probe counts as complete. |
| V-B | Evidence record for ACT-1…ACT-10 on disposable fixtures; real-Electron run with journal excerpt and before/after manifest diff; packaged run when a package exists; owner-data run only with recorded operation-specific consent; ledger row updated with SHA. |
| V-C | Evidence record for ACT and PRV tests with the simulated runner/mock provider; read-only real-host diagnostics where applicable; a real-effect run only on a disposable VM or with recorded owner consent, otherwise recorded `NOT RUN` and the status stays Partially implemented; ledger row updated. |
| V-D | All local layers (EXT-1…EXT-9 with mocks) pass and the service sits at `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL`; COMPLETE_VERIFIED only after a live, owner-consented run is recorded (time, account type, granted scopes, result). |
| L-H | Discover → Observe (measured) → Hand off (deep link with context, no approval token) → Record. The action lifecycle lives in the owning domain named in the block. |
| V-H | V-A plus HND-1…HND-3: the surface is complete when it observes accurately, hands off correctly and never mutates. |
| I-1 | One typed, versioned, allowlisted IPC contract from the existing workspace to Main and the provider; live state, errors, cancel and results rendered in the existing Living Glass workspace and inspector; no new generic modal, no duplicate architecture, no raw `ipcRenderer` in React, no generic exec endpoint. |
