## 42. Consolidated Blockers, Owner Decisions and Handover Obligations

This list replaces the scattered "needs owner" notes of Volumes 1–5. Every item names what is needed, who can supply it, and what Codex still completes without it. **No item below stops Codex from continuing with other work.** Items are recorded in `HANDOVER.md` under "Blocked on owner input" with the exact names the code uses (names, never secret values).

### 42.1 External or environmental blockers (not decisions)

| ID | Needed | Affects | Honest state until supplied |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-G1…B-G7 | Google Cloud project, OAuth client, test user, live consent, verification, security assessment (if required), Picker key/App ID, Workspace account | D04.01–D04.10, D14.03 | `needs-credential` / `needs-user-consent` / `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL`; see Section 37.7 |
| B-A1 | An AI provider key (or the owner's chosen provider), stored in a native vault; a secure provider proxy with consent and host allowlist | D14.02, D14.08, any AI assist in other domains | Controls disabled with the exact missing item; the existing session-only key path is not promoted |
| B-S1 | A Windows host **with a battery** (or recorded fixtures from one) | D10.26, D10.30, D13.09 live proof | Fixture-tested only; status stays Partially implemented |
| B-S2 | A host whose storage exposes reliability data and an owner-run elevated read | D07.09, D10.32, D10.31 (parts) | `unsupported-device` / `needs-elevation` states |
| B-W1 | A disposable Windows VM or test machine with administrator rights | PRV-5 for D11.03/.05/.07/.08/.12, D10.24, D12.02/.03, D09.02/.03/.11/.12, D05.06/.07/.08, D08.08 | Simulated-runner evidence only; real-effect tests recorded `NOT RUN` |
| B-W2 | WinGet on the owner host (App Installer) and a disposable package for one real install | D05.08 real execution | Mock-executor evidence only |
| B-R1 | A code-signing certificate and a signed-installer pipeline | Release layer (Volume 1 Section 10.1 layer 7) for all services | Unsigned packaged builds only |
| B-V1 | Owner visual sign-off ("gate 10"); manual native-dialog runs; broad real-media performance runs; RTL/scaling acceptance | D03.01–D03.09 and every UI | Functional scope preserved; visual stream remains Codex's, approval remains the owner's |
| B-O1 | Owner-data runs (organization, quarantine, restore on the owner's real files) | D06.02–D06.04, D07.02–D07.06, D07.10, D08.01–D08.05, D08.12 | Disposable-fixture evidence only |

### 42.2 Genuine product-policy decisions (with defaults Codex applies if the owner has not decided)

| ID | Decision | Default applied | Why a default is safe |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-1 | Accept WinGet source agreements: per invocation, per source (remembered), or never | **Explicit consent per source, remembered only if the user chooses "remember"; revocable** | No silent acceptance of legal terms |
| P-2 | Pursue public release of any sensitive/restricted Google scope (D04.02 Gmail read, D04.05 Contacts, others) and, for restricted scopes, the security assessment | **Owner-only (Testing status) use; feature flag off for general release** | Avoids an unreviewed public commitment; local work is complete either way |
| P-3 | Use `drive.file` + Picker to make Sheets non-sensitive, or keep the existing scopes | **Keep existing scopes; record and minimize them (37.2)** | No behavior change to a built provider |
| P-4 | Load a third-party hardware-monitor driver for deeper thermal data | **No** | Kernel-driver risk; honest empty state is acceptable |
| P-5 | Whether KNOuX may ever relaunch itself elevated | **No; OS-provided prompts only (Class C rule)** | Matches the standard document |
| P-6 | Whether "remember" source/agreement consent and scheduled actions may run unattended | **No unattended Class B/C execution on owner data** | Matches the real-service standard ("no blanket overnight execution on owner data") |

### 42.3 Destructive or irreversible actions that always need operation-specific consent at the time of use

Real application installs/upgrades (WinGet), uninstall and leftover removal, service state or startup-policy changes, startup entry changes, DISM/SFC repairs, DNS/Winsock/TCP-IP/Windows Update resets, restore-point creation or restore, permanent deletion, cloud-account connection and any charge to an external API. Codex builds and tests the executors against disposable fixtures and simulated runners, and never runs them against owner data or the owner's system without that recorded consent.

### 42.4 Handover obligations

At the end of each verified slice Codex updates the ledger row (`current_slice_status`, `implementation_sha`, `evidence`, `remaining_gate`) in `docs/LIVE_SERVICE_EXECUTION_STATUS_181.csv` and the matching rows in the implementation CSV, and appends to `HANDOVER.md`: the scoreboard per service with evidence paths, this blocker list with updates, items needing a Windows host, known limitations, and decisions taken. No row is promoted without an evidence record (Volume 1 Section 10.2).

**Method.** The 181 ledger rows were joined to the implementation CSV (by catalog ID), to the registry evidence (by catalog ID, or by the proposed alias in 33.2) and to the acceptance document (by catalog ID). Statuses follow the precedence in 32.5 and the mapping in 33.4. Spec references are positional within each domain and were verified name-by-name against the 181 headings of Volumes 1–5. Section 40 and the addenda in Section 41 are generated from those joins; each cell is copied or derived, none is invented. Where a cell has no recorded evidence it says so.
